### Slide 4
- Transformer
- A transformer is the simplest device that is used to transfer electrical energy from one alternating-current circuit to another circuit or multiple circuits through electromagnetic induction.
- 3
- Additional / Recommended Readings:
- https://stock.adobe.com/in/images/current-transformer-diagram-in-physics/500340344
- Figure. 1 Transformer

### Slide 5
- Types of Transformer
- There are primarily two types of Transformer based on the operating voltage. The following are some of them: A) Step Up B) Step Down
- 4
- Additional / Recommended Readings:
- https://www.geeksforgeeks.org/transformer/
- Figure. 2 Types of transformer

### Slide 6
- Step Up Transformer
- The secondary voltage of a step-up transformer is raised from the low primary voltage.
- Because the primary winding has fewer turns than the secondary winding in this sort of transformer, the ratio of the primary to secondary winding will be greater than one.
- 5

### Slide 7
- Step Down Transformer
- The primary voltage is converted to a lower voltage across the secondary output using a step-down transformer.
- The number of windings on the primary side of a step-down transformer is more than on the secondary side. As a result, the overall secondary-to-primary winding ratio will always be less than one.
- 6

### Slide 8
- Based on Core Material
- Iron Core Transformer:
- Multiple soft iron plates are used as the core of an iron core transformer. The iron's strong magnetic properties of the iron core transformer have extremely high flux linkage.
- Ferrite Core Transformer:
- Due to its high magnetic permeability, a ferrite core transformer uses one. In the high-frequency application, this kind of transformer provides incredibly low losses. In high-frequency applications like SMPS.
- 7

### Slide 9
- Based on Winding
- Auto Winding transformer:
- The primary and secondary windings have always been fixed, but with an auto-winding transformer, they can be connected in series, and the center-tapped node can be moved.
- The secondary voltage can be altered by changing the location of the central tap. The auto is used to alert the self or a single coil and is not the abbreviation for Automatic.
- 8

### Slide 10
- Parts of Transformer
- Core:
- The transformer core serves as a support for the winding. Additionally, it offers a magnetic flux flow channel with minimal resistance.
- Windings:
- The copper wires that are wound over the transformer core are known as windings. Copper cables are used because Copper's high conductivity reduces transformer loss because resistance to current flow lowers as conductivity rises.
- 9

### Slide 11
- Parts of Transformer
- Tank: A transformer main tank serves two purposes:
- The core and the windings are protected from the elements, such as rain and dust.
- It functions as an oil container as well as a support for all other transformer attachments.
- Breather:
- All oil-immersed transformer with conservator tank includes it.
- It aids in the protection of the oil against moisture.
- 10

### Slide 12
- Basic Principles
- of Transformer
- It works on the principle of Faraday’s Law of Electromagnetic Induction which states that “ the magnitude of voltage is directly proportional to the rate of change of flux.”
- 11
- Additional / Recommended Readings:
- https://circuitglobe.com/what-is-a-transformer.html
- Figure.3 transformer

### Slide 13
- Working Principles of Transformer
- When the AC supply is given to the primary winding with a voltage of V1, an alternating flux ϕ sets up in the core of the transformer, which links with the secondary winding and as a result of it, an emf is induced in it called Mutually Induced emf. The direction of this induced emf is opposite to the applied voltage V1, this is because of the Lenz’s law.
- 12

### Slide 14
- Working Principles of Transformer
- Physically, there is no electrical connection between the two windings, but they are magnetically connected.
- 13
- Additional / Recommended Readings:
- https://circuitglobe.com/working-principle-of-a-transformer.html
- Figure. 4 Working Principle of Transformer

### Slide 15
- Applications
- of Transformer
- Increasing or reducing the voltage level in an AC circuit to ensure the correct operation of the circuit's various electrical components.
- It stops DC from flowing from one circuit to another.
- It separates two separate electric circuits.
- Before transmission and distribution can take place, the voltage level at the electric power plant must be increased.
- 14

- 2. We have learnt about different types of transformer.
- 3. We have
- studied working principles of transformer.

### Slide 10
- Turn Ratio
- of Transformer
- It is defined as the ratio of primary to secondary turns.
- If N2 > N1 the transformer is called Step-up transformer
- If N2 < N1 the transformer is called Step down transformer
- 9

### Slide 11
- Transformation Ratio
- of Transformer
- The transformation ratio is defined as the ratio of the secondary voltage to the primary voltage. It is denoted by K.
- 10

### Slide 12
- Losses of Transformer
- There are various types of losses in the transformer such as iron loss, copper loss, hysteresis loss, eddy current loss, stray loss, and dielectric loss.
- The hysteresis losses occur because of the variation of the magnetization in the core of the transformer and the copper loss occurs because of the transformer winding resistance.
- 11

### Slide 13
- Losses of Transformer
- There are different types of losses as listed below:
- Iron Losses
- Hysteresis Loss
- Eddy Current Loss
- Copper Loss Or Ohmic Loss
- Stray Loss
- Dielectric Loss
- 12
- Additional / Recommended Readings:
- https://circuitglobe.com/types-of-losses-in-transformer.html
- Figure. 2 Losses of Transformer

### Slide 14
- Iron Losses
- Iron losses are caused by the alternating flux in the core of the transformer as this loss occurs in the core it is also known as Core loss. Iron loss is further divided into hysteresis and eddy current loss.
- Hysteresis Loss:
- The core of the transformer is subjected to an alternating magnetizing force, and for each cycle of emf, a hysteresis loop is traced out.
- 13

### Slide 15
- Iron Losses
- Power is dissipated in the form of heat known as hysteresis loss and given by the equation shown below:
- Where
- KȠ is a proportionality constant which depends upon the volume and quality of the material of the core used in the transformer,
- f is the supply frequency,
- Bmax
- is the maximum or peak value of the flux density.
- 14

### Slide 16
- Iron Losses
- Eddy Current Loss:
- When the flux links with a closed circuit, an emf is induced in the circuit and the current flows, the value of the current depends upon the amount of emf around the circuit and the resistance of the circuit.
- These circulating currents are called Eddy Currents.
- As these currents are not responsible for doing any useful work, and it produces a loss (I^2R loss) in the magnetic material known as an Eddy Current Loss.
- 15

### Slide 17
- Iron Losses
- The equation of the eddy current loss is given as:
- Where,
- Ke – coefficient of eddy current.
- Bm – maximum value of flux density in
- wb
- /m^2
- T – thickness of lamination in meters
- F – frequency of reversal of the magnetic field in Hz
- V – the volume of magnetic material in m^3
- 16

### Slide 18
- Copper
- Losses
- These losses occur due to ohmic resistance of the transformer windings. If I1 and I2 are the primary and the secondary current.
- R1 and R2 are the resistance of primary and secondary winding then the copper losses occurring in the primary and secondary winding will be I^2*R1 and I^2*R2 respectively.
- 17

### Slide 19
- Copper
- Losses
- Therefore, the total copper losses will be
- These losses varied according to the load and known hence it is also known as variable losses. Copper losses vary as the square of the load current.
- 18

### Slide 20
- Stray
- Losses
- The occurrence of these stray losses is due to the presence of leakage field.
- The percentage of these losses are very small as compared to the iron and copper losses so they can be neglected.
- 19

### Slide 21
- Dielectric Losses
- Dielectric loss occurs in the insulating material of the transformer that is in the oil of the transformer, or in the solid insulations.
- When the oil gets deteriorated or the solid insulation gets damaged, or its quality decreases, and because of this, the efficiency of the transformer gets affected.
- 20

### Course Information
# Course Information

The course Semiconductor Physics and Basic Electronic Circuits provides fundamental knowledge of semiconductor materials, electronic devices, and basic electrical circuit analysis. The course covers the concepts of intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, energy band theory, and charge carrier conduction. It includes detailed study of the PN junction diode, its formation, biasing, V-I characteristics, and applications, along with the Zener diode, Zener and avalanche breakdown mechanisms, and voltage regulation applications. The course also explains different types of rectifiers, including half-wave, full-wave, center-tapped, and bridge rectifiers, along with ripple factor and filter circuits. In the transistor section, students learn the construction and operation of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJT), including NPN and PNP transistors, transistor configurations such as CB, CE, and CC, and applications of transistors as amplifiers and switches. Basic circuit analysis techniques using Kirchhoff’s Current Law (KCL) and Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law (KVL) are introduced for solving electrical networks. The course further includes the study of transformers, covering construction, working principle, EMF equation, transformer losses, efficiency, and practical applications. Overall, the course develops theoretical understanding and practical skills in semiconductor devices and basic electronic circuits essential for electrical and electronics engineering students.

---


### Course Introduction, Objectives and Outcomes
# Course Introduction, Objectives and Outcomes

## Course Introduction:

In this subject students will learn basic fundamentals of semiconductor physics with its applications. Basically Semiconductor physics is the branch of physics that deals with the properties of semiconductor materials, especially how they conduct electricity under different conditions. Semiconductors are the backbone of modern electronics—used in computers, phones, solar cells, LEDs, and more.

In unit-1, we will focus on formation of pn junction diode with its characteristics and applications. they will focus on Basic Structure of P-N Junction, P-N Junction as a diode, Forward and reverse biasing of P-N Junction diode, Volt-Ampere Characteristics of P-N Junction Diode, Avalanche Breakdown,Zener Breakdown,Zener diode, Volt-ampere Characteristics of Zener Diode, Applications of P-N Junction and Zener diode. Further with the help of practical knowledge they will be able to design projects on semiconductor physics.

In unit-2, students will learn about transistor in which it contains Introduction to BJT: Formation of PNP / NPN junctions, Transistor Circuit Configuration: CB, CE, CC configuration ,Operating regions of Transistor, injection efficiency, base transport factor and current amplification factors, Transistor as a switch and an Amplifier. Similarly, another topic is Introduction to energy storage devices, Selecting Battery: Basic Battery Specifications, common parameters of battery/applications ,Different types of Batteries used in different applications, Power Supplies: Linear and SMPS.

In unit-3 students will learn about basic concepts of AC and DC circuits with applications of kirchhoff's laws. Introduction to AC circuits, Study of AC circuits of series RL, RC circuit corresponding Voltage-current Phasor diagram and their relation will be covered with basic construction and working principles of transformer.

## Course Objectives:

The course should enable the student to

1.      To describe the the basic materials and the fundamental properties of semiconductor materials, including intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, charge carriers, and PN junction behavior.

2.      To understand the construction, operation, and characteristics of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJTs), FETs and its applications.

3.      To apply the knowledge of semiconductor devices, transistors, electric circuits, and transformers to solve practical engineering problems in electronics, communication, and power systems.

## Course Outcomes:

CO.1 Describe the properties of materials and Application of semiconductor electronics devices such as diodes, Transistor.

CO.2 Examine the characteristics and operaing principles of various electronic devices includes diodes and transistors.

CO.3 Apply the relationships between the key parameters of diode, transistor configurations, to analyze electronics circuits.

CO.4 Illustrate the characteristics of diodes, different types of transistors and the basic concept of electric circuits.

CO.5 To Understand the fundamental laws to solve practical engineering problems of semiconductors, transistors, electric circuits, and transformers.

---

### Netiquette Guidelines
# Netiquette Guidelines

Netiquette is etiquette for the internet, guidelines and good practices for online communication in emails, discussion forums, and chat rooms.

Please practice the following guidelines when posting comments in the course:

- Format your text so it is easy to read. Try to use standard paragraph spacing, indentation, and legible font. Try to be consistent with your formatting, including font size, color, etc.

- Be concise and to the point in your communication. Limit discussion forum posts to a few paragraphs. Proofread and edit your work before posting.

- AVOID THE USE OF ALL CAPS as this may be interpreted as shouting. All lowercase letters also make text more difficult to read. Use standard punctuation. Remember what your teacher taught you in primary school about good punctuation and spelling.

- Avoid 'texting' abbreviations such as OMG.

- Avoid using humor and sarcasm, as they are often difficult to detect in written communication. It is okay to sparingly use emoticons to communicate subtleties or emotions that are more obvious in spoken communication.

- Flaming is not proper in our courses (a rant about someone or some topic). Practice civil discussion.

- Respect the privacy of communication within a course. Do not publicly forward the comments of classmates to others outside of the course.

- Stay on topic in a discussion.

- Be respectful.

- Participants have varying degrees of sensitivity, and that even some well-meaning constructive suggestions (e.g., comments about broken links, typo, and misspellings) may be misconstrued as public criticism. Therefore, please refrain from sharing comments in ways that might be misinterpreted, or do so privately when appropriate.

(Any violation to the above points if reported by the teacher may lead to disciplinary action against the student)

Netiquette is etiquette for the internet, guidelines and good practices for online communication in emails, discussion forums, and chat rooms.

Please practice the following guidelines when posting comments in the course:

- Format your text so it is easy to read. Try to use standard paragraph spacing, indentation, and legible font. Try to be consistent with your formatting, including font size, color, etc.

- Be concise and to the point in your communication. Limit discussion forum posts to a few paragraphs. Proofread and edit your work before posting.

- AVOID THE USE OF ALL CAPS as this may be interpreted as shouting. All lowercase letters also make text more difficult to read. Use standard punctuation. Remember what your teacher taught you in primary school about good punctuation and spelling.

- Avoid 'texting' abbreviations such as OMG.

- Avoid using humor and sarcasm, as they are often difficult to detect in written communication. It is okay to sparingly use emoticons to communicate subtleties or emotions that are more obvious in spoken communication.

- Flaming is not proper in our courses (a rant about someone or some topic). Practice civil discussion.

- Respect the privacy of communication within a course. Do not publicly forward the comments of classmates to others outside of the course.

- Stay on topic in a discussion.

- Be respectful.

- Participants have varying degrees of sensitivity, and that even some well-meaning constructive suggestions (e.g., comments about broken links, typo, and misspellings) may be misconstrued as public criticism. Therefore, please refrain from sharing comments in ways that might be misinterpreted, or do so privately when appropriate.

(Any violation to the above points if reported by the teacher may lead to disciplinary action against the student)

### Course Information
# Course Information

The course Semiconductor Physics and Basic Electronic Circuits provides fundamental knowledge of semiconductor materials, electronic devices, and basic electrical circuit analysis. The course covers the concepts of intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, energy band theory, and charge carrier conduction. It includes detailed study of the PN junction diode, its formation, biasing, V-I characteristics, and applications, along with the Zener diode, Zener and avalanche breakdown mechanisms, and voltage regulation applications. The course also explains different types of rectifiers, including half-wave, full-wave, center-tapped, and bridge rectifiers, along with ripple factor and filter circuits. In the transistor section, students learn the construction and operation of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJT), including NPN and PNP transistors, transistor configurations such as CB, CE, and CC, and applications of transistors as amplifiers and switches. Basic circuit analysis techniques using Kirchhoff’s Current Law (KCL) and Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law (KVL) are introduced for solving electrical networks. The course further includes the study of transformers, covering construction, working principle, EMF equation, transformer losses, efficiency, and practical applications. Overall, the course develops theoretical understanding and practical skills in semiconductor devices and basic electronic circuits essential for electrical and electronics engineering students.

---


### Course Introduction, Objectives and Outcomes
# Course Introduction, Objectives and Outcomes

## Course Introduction:

In this subject students will learn basic fundamentals of semiconductor physics with its applications. Basically Semiconductor physics is the branch of physics that deals with the properties of semiconductor materials, especially how they conduct electricity under different conditions. Semiconductors are the backbone of modern electronics—used in computers, phones, solar cells, LEDs, and more.

In unit-1, we will focus on formation of pn junction diode with its characteristics and applications. they will focus on Basic Structure of P-N Junction, P-N Junction as a diode, Forward and reverse biasing of P-N Junction diode, Volt-Ampere Characteristics of P-N Junction Diode, Avalanche Breakdown,Zener Breakdown,Zener diode, Volt-ampere Characteristics of Zener Diode, Applications of P-N Junction and Zener diode. Further with the help of practical knowledge they will be able to design projects on semiconductor physics.

In unit-2, students will learn about transistor in which it contains Introduction to BJT: Formation of PNP / NPN junctions, Transistor Circuit Configuration: CB, CE, CC configuration ,Operating regions of Transistor, injection efficiency, base transport factor and current amplification factors, Transistor as a switch and an Amplifier. Similarly, another topic is Introduction to energy storage devices, Selecting Battery: Basic Battery Specifications, common parameters of battery/applications ,Different types of Batteries used in different applications, Power Supplies: Linear and SMPS.

In unit-3 students will learn about basic concepts of AC and DC circuits with applications of kirchhoff's laws. Introduction to AC circuits, Study of AC circuits of series RL, RC circuit corresponding Voltage-current Phasor diagram and their relation will be covered with basic construction and working principles of transformer.

## Course Objectives:

The course should enable the student to

1.      To describe the the basic materials and the fundamental properties of semiconductor materials, including intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, charge carriers, and PN junction behavior.

2.      To understand the construction, operation, and characteristics of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJTs), FETs and its applications.

3.      To apply the knowledge of semiconductor devices, transistors, electric circuits, and transformers to solve practical engineering problems in electronics, communication, and power systems.

## Course Outcomes:

CO.1 Describe the properties of materials and Application of semiconductor electronics devices such as diodes, Transistor.

CO.2 Examine the characteristics and operaing principles of various electronic devices includes diodes and transistors.

CO.3 Apply the relationships between the key parameters of diode, transistor configurations, to analyze electronics circuits.

CO.4 Illustrate the characteristics of diodes, different types of transistors and the basic concept of electric circuits.

CO.5 To Understand the fundamental laws to solve practical engineering problems of semiconductors, transistors, electric circuits, and transformers.

## Theory:

A Zener diode is a special type of diode designed to operate in the reverse breakdown region without damage. It is a heavily doped silicon crystal diode which allows current to flow in the forward direction in the same manner as an ideal diode. It also permits the current to flow in the reverse direction when the voltage is above a certain value known as the breakdown voltage. Breakdown voltage is also known as Zener knee voltage.

The device was named after an American Physicist, Clarence Zener, who described the property concerning the breakdown of electrical insulators.

The device consists of a reverse biased, highly doped, p-n junction diode operating in the breakdown region. Conventional diodes and rectifiers never operate in the breakdown region, but the Zener diode can safely be operated at this point.

## Working of Zener diode:

As the reverse voltage applied to the Zener diode increases, it reaches the breakdown voltage at which Zener current increases to a large value. In the breakdown region, further increase in reverse voltage will not increase the voltage across the Zener diode, it only increases the current. Thus, a constant voltage called Zener voltage (Vz) is maintained across the Zener diode when the supply voltage changes. Hence, it acts as a voltage regulator.

The reverse characteristic is obtained by taking reverse voltage along – ve X-axis and reverse current along –ve Y-axis. As the reverse voltage reaches a certain value, the reverse current increases to a large value, but the voltage across the diode remains a constant. This is the break down voltage Vz.

In forward bias, it behaves like a regular diode.

In reverse bias, when the applied voltage reaches Zener breakdown voltage, it conducts and maintains a constant voltage.

This property is widely used for voltage regulation.

## Circuit Diagram:

Fig. 2: Zener diode in reverse bias(RB)

Procedure:

## A. Forward Bias:

Connect the Zener diode in forward bias.

Increase the input voltage from 0 V to about 1.0 V in steps.

Record the corresponding current values.

## B. Reverse Bias:

Connect the Zener diode in reverse bias.

Slowly increase the voltage from 0 V to beyond Zener voltage (e.g., 6 V).

Note the voltage and corresponding reverse current at each step.

Beyond Zener breakdown, observe that the voltage remains approximately constant.

##

##

## Graph:

You will draw the V-I curve on a graph paper or digitally. It has two parts:

## 1. Forward Bias Curve:

Similar to a normal diode.

Sharp increase after ~0.6–0.7 V.

## 2. Reverse Bias Curve:

Very small current until the Zener breakdown voltage (~6.2V).

After breakdown, the voltage stays nearly constant despite increasing current.

Characteristics of zener diode:

Fig.  V I Characteristics of zener diode

## Results:

The V-I characteristic of Zener diode indicates that characteristic of Zener diode in forward bias is same as PN junction diode. In reverse bias, a negligible constant current flow through the zener diode but the current becomes abruptly large at certain voltage. This voltage is called as zener voltage. This sudden and sharp increase in zener current is called as zener breakdown. Vknee=0.7 volt and VZ= 9 volt.

## Precautions:

1.  The connection should be tight otherwise fluctuation in voltage and current will happen.

2.  At the turning point of curve, more reading should be taken.

3.  For the plot of Graph, current should be taken mA for both forward and reverse biased diode.

4.  The reading should be in multiple of least count.

## Theory:

The conversion of AC into DC is called Rectification. Electronic devices can convert AC power into DC power with high efficiency. The efficiency of the Half Wave Rectifier is 40.6%.

Consider the given circuit. Assume the diode to be ideal i.e. Vf = 0,  Rr =infinite, Rs = 0.

During the positive half cycle, the diode is forward biased and it conducts and hence a current flows through the load resistor. During the negative half cycle, the diode is reverse biased and it is equivalent to an open circuit, hence the current through the load resistance is zero.

Thus the diode conducts only for one half cycle and results in a half wave rectified output.

During positive half-cycle of the input voltage, the diode D1 is in forward bias and conducts    through the load resistor RL. Hence the current produces an output voltage across the load resistor RL, which has the same shape as the +ve half cycle of the input voltage.

During the negative half-cycle of the input voltage, the diode is reverse biased and there is no current through the circuit. i.e., the voltage across RL is zero. The net result is that only the +ve half cycle of the input voltage appears across the load. The average value of the half wave rectified o/p voltage is the value measured on dc voltmeter.

For practical circuits, transformer coupling is usually provided for two reasons.

1. The voltage can be stepped-up or stepped-down, as needed.

2. The ac source is electrically isolated from the rectifier. Thus preventing shock hazards in the secondary circuit.

A Half Wave Rectifier allows only one half-cycle (positive or negative) of the AC input to pass, blocking the other. The output is a pulsating DC.

A filter (typically a capacitor) is used to reduce ripples and smooth the output.

Ripple Factor (γ) is defined as:

Ripple Factor (γ)=Vrms (AC)VDC\text{Ripple Factor (γ)} = \frac{V_{\text{rms (AC)}}}{V_{\text{DC}}}

For ideal DC, ripple factor = 0.

## Circuit Diagrams:

1. Half Wave Rectifier (Without Filter):

Fig.1 Half wave rectifier without filter

Fig.2 Half wave rectifier with filter

## Procedure:

A. Without Filter:

Connect the circuit as per the HWR diagram without a capacitor.

Connect the primary of the transformer to AC mains ac mains and the secondary side to the rectifier input.

By the multimeter, measure the ac input voltage of the rectifier and, ac and dc voltage at the output of the rectifier.

Observe the output waveform across the load resistor using a CRO.

Measure the DC voltage and AC voltage (ripple) across the load using a multimeter or CRO.

Find the theoretical value of dc voltage by using the formula, Vdc=Vm/П

Where, Vm=2Vrms, (Vrms=output ac voltage.)

The Ripple factor is calculated by using the formula

r = ac output voltage/dc output voltage.

## B. With Filter:

Add a capacitor in parallel with the load resistor.

Again, observe the output waveform.

Measure the DC output voltage and the ripple voltage.

Note how the capacitor smoothens the waveform.

##

MODEL WAVEFORMS:

Outcomes: Students are able to

- analyze the operation of Half Wave rectifier with and without filter.

- calculate its performance parameters-ripple factor, percentage regulation, efficiency with and without filter.

## Result:

The Half Wave Rectifier successfully converts AC to pulsating DC.

The addition of a capacitor significantly reduces ripple.

Ripple factor is high without a filter and much lower with a filter.

## Precautions:

Use the capacitor with correct polarity and voltage rating.

Do not touch circuit components when powered.

Ensure proper connections before switching on the supply.

Discharge capacitor safely before handling.

### Course Information
# Course Information

The course Semiconductor Physics and Basic Electronic Circuits provides fundamental knowledge of semiconductor materials, electronic devices, and basic electrical circuit analysis. The course covers the concepts of intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, energy band theory, and charge carrier conduction. It includes detailed study of the PN junction diode, its formation, biasing, V-I characteristics, and applications, along with the Zener diode, Zener and avalanche breakdown mechanisms, and voltage regulation applications. The course also explains different types of rectifiers, including half-wave, full-wave, center-tapped, and bridge rectifiers, along with ripple factor and filter circuits. In the transistor section, students learn the construction and operation of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJT), including NPN and PNP transistors, transistor configurations such as CB, CE, and CC, and applications of transistors as amplifiers and switches. Basic circuit analysis techniques using Kirchhoff’s Current Law (KCL) and Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law (KVL) are introduced for solving electrical networks. The course further includes the study of transformers, covering construction, working principle, EMF equation, transformer losses, efficiency, and practical applications. Overall, the course develops theoretical understanding and practical skills in semiconductor devices and basic electronic circuits essential for electrical and electronics engineering students.

---


### Course Introduction, Objectives and Outcomes
# Course Introduction, Objectives and Outcomes

## Course Introduction:

In this subject students will learn basic fundamentals of semiconductor physics with its applications. Basically Semiconductor physics is the branch of physics that deals with the properties of semiconductor materials, especially how they conduct electricity under different conditions. Semiconductors are the backbone of modern electronics—used in computers, phones, solar cells, LEDs, and more.

In unit-1, we will focus on formation of pn junction diode with its characteristics and applications. they will focus on Basic Structure of P-N Junction, P-N Junction as a diode, Forward and reverse biasing of P-N Junction diode, Volt-Ampere Characteristics of P-N Junction Diode, Avalanche Breakdown,Zener Breakdown,Zener diode, Volt-ampere Characteristics of Zener Diode, Applications of P-N Junction and Zener diode. Further with the help of practical knowledge they will be able to design projects on semiconductor physics.

In unit-2, students will learn about transistor in which it contains Introduction to BJT: Formation of PNP / NPN junctions, Transistor Circuit Configuration: CB, CE, CC configuration ,Operating regions of Transistor, injection efficiency, base transport factor and current amplification factors, Transistor as a switch and an Amplifier. Similarly, another topic is Introduction to energy storage devices, Selecting Battery: Basic Battery Specifications, common parameters of battery/applications ,Different types of Batteries used in different applications, Power Supplies: Linear and SMPS.

In unit-3 students will learn about basic concepts of AC and DC circuits with applications of kirchhoff's laws. Introduction to AC circuits, Study of AC circuits of series RL, RC circuit corresponding Voltage-current Phasor diagram and their relation will be covered with basic construction and working principles of transformer.

## Course Objectives:

The course should enable the student to

1.      To describe the the basic materials and the fundamental properties of semiconductor materials, including intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, charge carriers, and PN junction behavior.

2.      To understand the construction, operation, and characteristics of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJTs), FETs and its applications.

3.      To apply the knowledge of semiconductor devices, transistors, electric circuits, and transformers to solve practical engineering problems in electronics, communication, and power systems.

## Course Outcomes:

CO.1 Describe the properties of materials and Application of semiconductor electronics devices such as diodes, Transistor.

CO.2 Examine the characteristics and operaing principles of various electronic devices includes diodes and transistors.

CO.3 Apply the relationships between the key parameters of diode, transistor configurations, to analyze electronics circuits.

CO.4 Illustrate the characteristics of diodes, different types of transistors and the basic concept of electric circuits.

CO.5 To Understand the fundamental laws to solve practical engineering problems of semiconductors, transistors, electric circuits, and transformers.

### Netiquette Guidelines
# Netiquette Guidelines

Netiquette is etiquette for the internet, guidelines and good practices for online communication in emails, discussion forums, and chat rooms.

Please practice the following guidelines when posting comments in the course:

- Format your text so it is easy to read. Try to use standard paragraph spacing, indentation, and legible font. Try to be consistent with your formatting, including font size, color, etc.

- Be concise and to the point in your communication. Limit discussion forum posts to a few paragraphs. Proofread and edit your work before posting.

- AVOID THE USE OF ALL CAPS as this may be interpreted as shouting. All lowercase letters also make text more difficult to read. Use standard punctuation. Remember what your teacher taught you in primary school about good punctuation and spelling.

- Avoid 'texting' abbreviations such as OMG.

- Avoid using humor and sarcasm, as they are often difficult to detect in written communication. It is okay to sparingly use emoticons to communicate subtleties or emotions that are more obvious in spoken communication.

- Flaming is not proper in our courses (a rant about someone or some topic). Practice civil discussion.

- Respect the privacy of communication within a course. Do not publicly forward the comments of classmates to others outside of the course.

- Stay on topic in a discussion.

- Be respectful.

- Participants have varying degrees of sensitivity, and that even some well-meaning constructive suggestions (e.g., comments about broken links, typo, and misspellings) may be misconstrued as public criticism. Therefore, please refrain from sharing comments in ways that might be misinterpreted, or do so privately when appropriate.

# Experiment-1.1

CO.1: Describe the properties of materials and Application of semiconductor electronics devices such as diodes and Transistor.

## Theory:

Donor impurities (penta-valent) are introduced into one-side and acceptor impurities into the other side of a single crystal of an intrinsic semiconductor to form a p-n diode with a Junction called depletion region (this region is depleted off the charge carriers). This Region gives rise to a potential barrier Vf called Cut- in Voltage. This is the voltage across the diode at which it starts conducting. It can conduct beyond this Potential.

The P-N junction supports uni-directional current flow. If +ve terminal of the input supply is connected to anode (P-side) and –ve terminal of the input supply is connected to cathode (N- side) then diode is said to be forward biased. In this condition the height of the potential barrier at the junction is lowered by an amount equal to given forward biasing voltage. Both the holes from p-side and electrons from n-side cross the junction simultaneously and constitute a forward current (injected minority current – due to holes crossing the junction and entering N-side of the diode, due to electrons crossing the junction and entering P-side of the diode).

This condition an amount equal to reverse biasing voltage increases the height of the potential barrier at the junction. Both the holes on p-side and electrons on n-side tend to move away from the junction thereby increasing the depleted region. However, the process cannot continue indefinitely, thus a small current called reverse saturation current continues to flow in the diode. This small current is due to thermally generated carriers. Assuming current flowing through the diode to be negligible, the diode can be approximated as an open circuited switch.

The volt-ampere characteristics of a diode explained by following equation:

where

I=current flowing in the diode

I0=reverse saturation current

V=voltage applied to the diode

VT=volt-equivalent of temperature=kT/q=T/11,600=26mV (@ room temp).

h=1 (for Ge) and 2 (for Si)

It is observed that Ge diode has smaller cut-in-voltage when compared to Si diode. The reverse saturation current in Ge diode is larger in magnitude when compared to silicon diode.

Reverse biased condition:

1.     Connect the circuit as shown in figure (2) using silicon PN Junction diode.

2.     Vary Vr gradually in steps of 0.5 volts up to 8 volts and note down the corresponding readings of Ir.

3.     Tabulate different reverse currents obtained for different reverse voltages. (Ir = VR / R, where VR is the Voltage across 10KW Resistor).

## Calculations from Graph:

Calculations from Graph:

- Cutin Voltage =  Vγ

- Static forward Resistance  Rdc = Vf/If Ω

- Dynamic Forward Resistance rac = Δ Vf/ΔIf Ω

- Static Reverse Resistance Rdc = Vr/Ir Ω

- Dynamic Reverse Resistance rac = Δ Vr/ΔIr Ω

### Experiment-2.3
# Experiment-2.3

CO.2 Explain the characteristics and operating principles of various electronic devices including diodes and transistors.

Manual of experiment:

Aim: To analyze the performance of Transistor as Switch.

Apparatus Required:

Theory:

A Transistor switch, which is used for opening or closing of a circuit that means the transistor, is commonly used as a switch in the electronic devices only for the low voltage applications because of its low power consumption. Transistor work as a switch when it is in cutoff and saturation regions. In this article, we will discuss how to use a transistor as a switch.

Circuit Diagram:

Fig.1 Transistor as a switch

Procedure:

1. Connect the circuit that shown in the figure as shown above.

2. Set the AC source (4V) and frequency (200 HZ) (Square wave) and draw it.

3. Set the oscilloscope (first terminal) at the common terminal of collector and resistance (RL) and (second terminal of OSC) to the emitter of transistor.

Operating Regions:

We can observe from the  characteristics, the pink shaded area at the bottom of the curves represents the Cut-off region and the blue area to the left represent the Saturation region of the transistor. These transistor regions are defined as

Fig.2 Characteristics of Transistor As A Switch

Cut-off Region: The operating conditions of the transistor are zero input base current (IB=0), zero output collector current(Ic=0), and maximum collector voltage (VCE) which results in a large depletion layer and no current flowing through the device. Therefore, the transistor is switched to “Fully-OFF”. So we can define the cut-off region when using a bipolar transistor as a switch as being, bother the junctions of NPN transistors are reverse biased, VB< 0.7v and Ic=0. Similarly, for PNP transistor, the emitter potential must be –ve with respect to the base of the transistor.

Saturation Region: In this region, the transistor will be biased so that the maximum amount of base current (IB) is applied, resulting in maximum collector current(IC=VCC/RL) and then resulting in the minimum collector-emitter voltage (VCE ~ 0) drop. At this condition, the depletion layer becomes as small as the possible and maximum current flowing through the transistor. Therefore, the transistor is switched “Fully-ON”.

Observation Table:

S.No.

Base Voltage (Vb)

Collector Voltage (Vc)

LED State

Transistor Region

1

0V

OFF

Cutoff

2

Low (~0.2V)

ON

Saturation

Calculations (if applicable):

Base current (Ib) = (Vinput – Vbe) / Rb

Collector current (Ic) ≈ (Vcc – Vce) / Rc

Check if Ic ≈ β × Ib (only in active region)

But for switching mode:

Focus on ensuring Vce(sat) ≈ 0.2V in ON state

And Ic ≈ 0 in OFF state

Result:

The experiment successfully demonstrates the working of a transistor as a switch. The transistor remains OFF in the absence of base current and turns ON when a sufficient base current is provided.

### Course Information
# Course Information

The course Semiconductor Physics and Basic Electronic Circuits provides fundamental knowledge of semiconductor materials, electronic devices, and basic electrical circuit analysis. The course covers the concepts of intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, energy band theory, and charge carrier conduction. It includes detailed study of the PN junction diode, its formation, biasing, V-I characteristics, and applications, along with the Zener diode, Zener and avalanche breakdown mechanisms, and voltage regulation applications. The course also explains different types of rectifiers, including half-wave, full-wave, center-tapped, and bridge rectifiers, along with ripple factor and filter circuits. In the transistor section, students learn the construction and operation of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJT), including NPN and PNP transistors, transistor configurations such as CB, CE, and CC, and applications of transistors as amplifiers and switches. Basic circuit analysis techniques using Kirchhoff’s Current Law (KCL) and Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law (KVL) are introduced for solving electrical networks. The course further includes the study of transformers, covering construction, working principle, EMF equation, transformer losses, efficiency, and practical applications. Overall, the course develops theoretical understanding and practical skills in semiconductor devices and basic electronic circuits essential for electrical and electronics engineering students.

---


### Course Introduction, Objectives and Outcomes
# Course Introduction, Objectives and Outcomes

## Course Introduction:

In this subject students will learn basic fundamentals of semiconductor physics with its applications. Basically Semiconductor physics is the branch of physics that deals with the properties of semiconductor materials, especially how they conduct electricity under different conditions. Semiconductors are the backbone of modern electronics—used in computers, phones, solar cells, LEDs, and more.

In unit-1, we will focus on formation of pn junction diode with its characteristics and applications. they will focus on Basic Structure of P-N Junction, P-N Junction as a diode, Forward and reverse biasing of P-N Junction diode, Volt-Ampere Characteristics of P-N Junction Diode, Avalanche Breakdown,Zener Breakdown,Zener diode, Volt-ampere Characteristics of Zener Diode, Applications of P-N Junction and Zener diode. Further with the help of practical knowledge they will be able to design projects on semiconductor physics.

In unit-2, students will learn about transistor in which it contains Introduction to BJT: Formation of PNP / NPN junctions, Transistor Circuit Configuration: CB, CE, CC configuration ,Operating regions of Transistor, injection efficiency, base transport factor and current amplification factors, Transistor as a switch and an Amplifier. Similarly, another topic is Introduction to energy storage devices, Selecting Battery: Basic Battery Specifications, common parameters of battery/applications ,Different types of Batteries used in different applications, Power Supplies: Linear and SMPS.

In unit-3 students will learn about basic concepts of AC and DC circuits with applications of kirchhoff's laws. Introduction to AC circuits, Study of AC circuits of series RL, RC circuit corresponding Voltage-current Phasor diagram and their relation will be covered with basic construction and working principles of transformer.

## Course Objectives:

The course should enable the student to

1.      To describe the the basic materials and the fundamental properties of semiconductor materials, including intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, charge carriers, and PN junction behavior.

2.      To understand the construction, operation, and characteristics of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJTs), FETs and its applications.

3.      To apply the knowledge of semiconductor devices, transistors, electric circuits, and transformers to solve practical engineering problems in electronics, communication, and power systems.

## Course Outcomes:

CO.1 Describe the properties of materials and Application of semiconductor electronics devices such as diodes, Transistor.

CO.2 Examine the characteristics and operaing principles of various electronic devices includes diodes and transistors.

CO.3 Apply the relationships between the key parameters of diode, transistor configurations, to analyze electronics circuits.

CO.4 Illustrate the characteristics of diodes, different types of transistors and the basic concept of electric circuits.

CO.5 To Understand the fundamental laws to solve practical engineering problems of semiconductors, transistors, electric circuits, and transformers.

### Netiquette Guidelines
# Netiquette Guidelines

Netiquette is etiquette for the internet, guidelines and good practices for online communication in emails, discussion forums, and chat rooms.

Please practice the following guidelines when posting comments in the course:

- Format your text so it is easy to read. Try to use standard paragraph spacing, indentation, and legible font. Try to be consistent with your formatting, including font size, color, etc.

- Be concise and to the point in your communication. Limit discussion forum posts to a few paragraphs. Proofread and edit your work before posting.

- AVOID THE USE OF ALL CAPS as this may be interpreted as shouting. All lowercase letters also make text more difficult to read. Use standard punctuation. Remember what your teacher taught you in primary school about good punctuation and spelling.

- Avoid 'texting' abbreviations such as OMG.

- Avoid using humor and sarcasm, as they are often difficult to detect in written communication. It is okay to sparingly use emoticons to communicate subtleties or emotions that are more obvious in spoken communication.

- Flaming is not proper in our courses (a rant about someone or some topic). Practice civil discussion.

- Respect the privacy of communication within a course. Do not publicly forward the comments of classmates to others outside of the course.

- Stay on topic in a discussion.

- Be respectful.

- Participants have varying degrees of sensitivity, and that even some well-meaning constructive suggestions (e.g., comments about broken links, typo, and misspellings) may be misconstrued as public criticism. Therefore, please refrain from sharing comments in ways that might be misinterpreted, or do so privately when appropriate.

(Any violation to the above points if reported by the teacher may lead to disciplinary action against the student)

### Experiment-2.1
# Experiment-2.1

CO.1 Describe the properties of materials and the applications of semiconductor electronic devices such as diodes and transistors.

Manual of experiment:

Aim: To analyze the performance of Transistor as CE configuration.

Apparatus Required:

Theory: The configuration in which the emitter is connected between the collector and base is known as a common emitter configuration. The input circuit is connected between emitter and base, and the output circuit is taken from the collector and emitter. Thus, the emitter is common to both the input and the output circuit, and hence the name is the common emitter configuration.

## 📘 LECTURE NOTES & EXPLANATIONS


### Experiment-3.2
# Experiment-3.2

CO.4 To Illustrate the characteristics of diodes, various types of transistors and

concept of electric circuits.

Manual of Experiment:

Aim: To Verify Kirchhoff’s

current Law.

Apparatus:

Theory:  It is an application of the principle of conservation of electric charge. The law states that at any circuit junction, the sum of the currents flowing into and out of that junction are equal.

The sum of all currents entering a node is equal to the sum of all currents leaving the node.

Circuit Diagram:

Fig.1 KCL Circuit

Observation Table:

Calculation: [According to circuit dig.]

I1= I2+ I4

I4 = I5+ I36

Learning Outcome:

Understand the Principle

Explain Kirchhoff’s Current Law as a consequence of charge conservation.

State that the total current entering a junction equals the total current leaving it.

Identify Nodes in a Circuit

Recognize nodes (junction points) in electrical circuits where currents meet or split.

Apply KCL to Solve Circuits

Write current equations at nodes using KCL.

Calculate unknown branch currents using KCL equations.

Analyze Parallel and Complex Circuits

Use KCL in analyzing parallel circuits and node-based networks.

Combine KCL with Ohm’s Law for solving complex circuits.

Verify and Troubleshoot Circuits

Confirm the correctness of current distribution in a circuit.

Use KCL for diagnosing faults in electrical networks.

Integrate with KVL for Full Analysis

Use KCL along with Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law (KVL) in systematic circuit analysis methods like nodal analysis.

Result: We have verified KCL.

Relevant Demonstrative Video LINK:

- Relevant Book/Standard Code: https://electricalnotebook.com/verification-of-kcl-kvl/

- Other Resource materials or Web Links: https://www.electronics-tutorials.ws/dccircuits/kirchhoffs-current-law.html

### Slide 3
- Kirchhoff’s Laws
- Kirchhoff's Laws are the basic laws used in electrostatics to solve complex circuit questions.
- There are  two laws Kirchhoff’s Current Law and Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law.
- These laws deal with the flow of current and the voltage applied in the complex circuit and provide a way to solve these complex circuits.
- 2

### Slide 4
- Kirchhoff’s Current Law
- Kirchhoff’s Current Law states that” the algebraic sum of all the currents at any node point or a junction of a circuit is zero”.
- Σ I = 0
- Additional / Recommended Readings:
- https://circuitglobe.com/what-is-kirchhoffs-current-law-and-kirchhoffs-voltage-law.html
- Figure.1 KCL Law
- 3

### Slide 5
- Sign Conventions for KCL
- Take Positive sign for current entering the junction and negative for current leaving the junction.
- If the looping direction is the same as the current flowing through the circuit, the voltage drop across the resistor is considered negative.

- Applications of Kirchhoff’s Laws
- It is used to find out how much current is flowing and how much voltage is dropped in various areas of the complicated circuit.
- It aids in determining the direction of current in various circuit loops.
- Kirchhoff's Laws can help you comprehend how energy moves via an electric circuit.

# Experiment-3.3 - Complete Study Material


## 📘 LECTURE NOTES & EXPLANATIONS


### Experiment-3.3
# Experiment-3.3

CO.2 To Understand the characteristics of different electronic devices such as diodes,

transistors.

Manual of Experiment:

Aim: To find the Voltage Current

relation in R-L series circuit and determine Power Factor.

Apparatus:

Resistor (R) – Known resistance value (in ohms)

Inductor (L) – Known inductance value (in henrys)

AC Power Supply – Typically low-voltage sinusoidal source

Connecting Wires – For circuit connections

Ammeter (AC) – To measure current in the circuit

Voltmeter (AC) – To measure voltage across resistor and inductor

Multimeter (optional) – For cross-checking measurements

Breadboard or Circuit Board – For assembling the components

Switch (optional) – To control the circuit powerTheory: A circuit that contains a pure resistance R ohms connected in series with a coil having a pure inductance of L (Henry) is known as RL Series Circuit. When an AC supply voltage V is applied, the current, I flows in the circuit.

Circuit Diagram:

Fig.1 Series RL Circuit

Observation Table:

Calculation:

Power Factor= P/V.I

Learning Outcomes:

Understand the Behavior of RL Circuits

Describe how current and voltage behave in a series RL circuit under AC supply.

Understand the concept of phase difference between voltage and current.

Measure and Analyze Circuit Parameters

Accurately measure voltage across the resistor and inductor using a voltmeter.

Calculate current using an ammeter or Ohm's Law.

Calculate Impedance and Phase Angle

Determine the total impedance of the RL circuit.

Calculate and interpret the phase angle between voltage and current.

Apply Theoretical Formulas to Practical Setup

Verify the relationship: V=(VR)2+(VL)2V = \sqrt{(VR)^2 + (VL)^2}​

Use vector diagrams (phasors) to analyze the circuit.

Relate to Real-World Applications

Understand how RL circuits are used in filters, timers, and inductive loads like motors.

Result: We have designed series RL circuit and have determined power factor of the circuit.

Relevant Demonstrative Video LINK:

- Relevant Book/Standard Code: https://circuitglobe.com/what-is-rl-series-circuit.html

- Other Resource materials or Web Links: https://www.eeeguide.com/series-circuit/

The course Semiconductor Physics and Basic Electronic Circuits provides fundamental knowledge of semiconductor materials, electronic devices, and basic electrical circuit analysis. The course covers the concepts of intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors, energy band theory, and charge carrier conduction. It includes detailed study of the PN junction diode, its formation, biasing, V-I characteristics, and applications, along with the Zener diode, Zener and avalanche breakdown mechanisms, and voltage regulation applications. The course also explains different types of rectifiers, including half-wave, full-wave, center-tapped, and bridge rectifiers, along with ripple factor and filter circuits. In the transistor section, students learn the construction and operation of Bipolar Junction Transistors (BJT), including NPN and PNP transistors, transistor configurations such as CB, CE, and CC, and applications of transistors as amplifiers and switches. Basic circuit analysis techniques using Kirchhoff’s Current Law (KCL) and Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law (KVL) are introduced for solving electrical networks. The course further includes the study of transformers, covering construction, working principle, EMF equation, transformer losses, efficiency, and practical applications. Netiquette is etiquette for the internet, guidelines and good practices for online communication in emails, discussion forums, and chat rooms.

Please practice the following guidelines when posting comments in the course:

- Format your text so it is easy to read. Try to use standard paragraph spacing, indentation, and legible font. Try to be consistent with your formatting, including font size, color, etc.

- Be concise and to the point in your communication. Limit discussion forum posts to a few paragraphs. Proofread and edit your work before posting.

- AVOID THE USE OF ALL CAPS as this may be interpreted as shouting. All lowercase letters also make text more difficult to read. Use standard punctuation. Remember what your teacher taught you in primary school about good punctuation and spelling.

- Avoid 'texting' abbreviations such as OMG.

- Avoid using humor and sarcasm, as they are often difficult to detect in written communication. It is okay to sparingly use emoticons to communicate subtleties or emotions that are more obvious in spoken communication.

- Flaming is not proper in our courses (a rant about someone or some topic). Practice civil discussion.

- Respect the privacy of communication within a course. Do not publicly forward the comments of classmates to others outside of the course.

- Stay on topic in a discussion.

- Be respectful.

- Participants have varying degrees of sensitivity, and that even some well-meaning constructive suggestions (e.g., comments about broken links, typo, and misspellings) may be misconstrued as public criticism. Therefore, please refrain from sharing comments in ways that might be misinterpreted, or do so privately when appropriate.

(Any violation to the above points if reported by the teacher may lead to disciplinary action against the student)

# Unit-3 (Practical) - Complete Study Material


## 📘 LECTURE NOTES & EXPLANATIONS


### Experiment-3.1
# Experiment-3.1

CO.4 To Illustrate the characteristics of diodes, various types of transistors and

concept of electric circuits.

Manual of Experiment:

Aim: To Verify Kirchhoff’s

voltage Law

Apparatus:

Theory:

The Kirchhoff Voltage Law KVL states that the algebraic sum of voltage produced and the voltage dropped in a closed loop (a closed path) of an electric circuit is always equal.

Circuit Diagram:

Fig.1 KVL Circuit

Observation Table:

Calculation:

V= V1 +V4

V4 = V2 +V5

V5= V3 +V6

Learning outcomes:

Understand the Principle

Explain Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law and its basis in energy conservation.

Recognize that the sum of voltage drops around any closed loop in a circuit is zero.

Apply KVL to Electrical Circuits

Identify loops in a circuit and apply KVL to write voltage equations.

Solve for unknown voltages or currents using KVL equations.

Analyze Series and Complex Circuits

Use KVL in analyzing simple series circuits.

Combine KVL with Ohm’s Law to solve more complex circuits (e.g., series-parallel networks).

Verify Circuit Behavior

Check the correctness of circuit operation by verifying KVL.

Understand and troubleshoot real-world circuits using KVL.

Integrate with Other Circuit Laws

Apply KVL together with Kirchhoff’s Current Law (KCL) for full circuit analysis.

Understand its role in mesh analysis.

Result: We have verified KVL law.

Relevant Demonstrative Video LINK:

- Relevant Book/Standard Code: https://www.allaboutcircuits.com/textbook/direct-current/chpt-6/kirchhoffs-voltage-law-kvl/

- Other Resource materials or Web Links: https://electric-shocks.com/kirchhoffs-voltage-law-kvl/

### Slide 3
- Kirchhoff’s Laws
- Kirchhoff's Laws are the basic laws used in electrostatics to solve complex circuit questions.
- There are  two laws Kirchhoff’s Current Law and Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law.
- These laws deal with the flow of current and the voltage applied in the complex circuit and provide a way to solve these complex circuits.
- 2

### Slide 4
- Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law
- Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law states that the algebraic sum of the voltages (or voltage drops) in any closed path of a network that is transverse in a single direction is zero.
- Additional / Recommended Readings:
- https://circuitglobe.com/what-is-kirchhoffs-current-law-and-kirchhoffs-voltage-law.html
- Figure.1 KVL Law
- 3

### Slide 5
- Sign Conventions for KVL
- Take Positive sign for current entering the junction and negative for current leaving the junction
- In a loop, a rise in potential difference or EMF from lower to higher is always seen as positive i.e. moving from negative to positive terminal
- In a loop, a reduction in potential difference or EMF from higher to lower is always seen as negative i.e. moving from positive to negative terminal.
- 4

### Slide 8
- Applications of Kirchhoff’s Laws
- It is used to find out how much current is flowing and how much voltage is dropped in various areas of the complicated circuit.
- It aids in determining the direction of current in various circuit loops.
- Kirchhoff's Laws can help you comprehend how energy moves via an electric circuit.
